As a result of these earthquakes, the central and centralized villages of Sagittarius, Dağönu, Molakamam, Agrets, Stews, and Uzyurt were completely destroyed, and 40,000 households were unusable. In the October 23rd earthquake, 601 people died in central Van and Ercish districts and villages in the region. 27 buildings were destroyed at the center of Van, where the November 9th earthquake was severely damaged, and 43 of our people were killed. It is known that the earthquake occurred as it entered the winter, which left homeless and could not enter their homes for fear of earthquakes, causing various difficulties in meeting the needs of our citizens' shelter in cold weather. Because of the lack of tents distributed in the early days of the earthquake, a large number of the people remained out in the open for days in the cold air. It is widely said that small tents distributed by the Red Moon and the AFAD have also been distributed in certain areas. The situation, which was also subject to visual and written media, continued for days. Some of the people settled in tent cities weeks later faced many negative conditions because of cold weather, and 12 of our people lost their lives in the fires in these tents. The people who spent most of the winter in tents were transferred to container cities near spring. In addition to the earthquake, Van's reconstruction of the earthquake had many setbacks in construction of earthquake dwellings, which the Prime Minister and TOKI co-organised for the permanent housing of the homeless. On the other hand, the sale of these dwellings to more than the cost of earthquake victims is a sign that, with the purchase of $1,200 from the owners of the environment arrangements following the end of these constructions, the TOKİ has increased its dwellings by increasing its costs to its rightful owners and that its land is mortgaged for the collapse of its dwellings; this earthquake is seen by the government as a "ticoarirant." It is not known how much of the work Van received after the earthquake was done because the public did not have clear information about how much financial aid was collected for the victims during the earthquake. This increasing solid waste not only fills high-value empty spaces, but also causes pollution of air, water and soil. The situation on Earth has also sparked the European Union. The waste prevention and management programme must be improved in our country. The European Union Action Plan is of great importance in bringing waste into the economy. Recycling needs to change the production and consumer habits of our country. In this regard, applications such as waste prevention, waste reduction, reuse, recycling, recycling of energy and disposal should be activated. The disposal of waste should be reduced as much as possible in our country. Waste storage should be very careful with its choice of location. Places that are inappropriate should not be allowed and placement should be carefully chosen. Papers that go to the trash should be prepared to recycle before the value is lost. The raw material must be recycled from the paper based entirely on imports. Paper and pet packaging should not be used to obtain energy anywhere. Waste paper should definitely go into recycling. Recycling products are involved in a large group of sales points and citizens that are being released in packaging. In almost every sector, many products are sold in packaging. In our country, the production and consumption of packaging materials are growing in parallel to industrial development, and the waste that we call useless after consuming the product, and the waste that we dump in the waste is the common property of society. So waste waste is not only considered property of municipalities and the original capital. Because recycling is a separate industrial branch. These packaging wastes should be transformed into other products in recycling facilities. For example, milk cartons are used in the furniture industry, pet wrappings are used in textiles, aluminum boxes are used in the white goods industry. The recycling sector contributes to the country's healthy and secure growth, preventing waste, reducing imports, increase in revenue, public health, conservation of nature, energy savings, and the longer life expectancy of its underground wealth. With support and incentives for the emerging recycling sector in our country, new technology and RD work must be launched.
